theme · 841 papers

Learning Theory

Why learning works at all: generalization, sample complexity, and guarantees.

← all themes

ICLR 2026

Larger Datasets Can Be Repeated More: A Theoretical Analysis of Multi-Epoch Scaling in Linear Regression

Problem. LLM training processes vast corpora in single pass, leaving available data underutilized, and the tradeoff between repeated epochs on same data versus fresh samples is theoretically unclear.
Approach. Theoretical analysis characterizes scaling behavior of effective data reuse rate E(K,N) for multi-epoch training using SGD in linear regression under strong convexity or Zipf-distributed data.
Contribution. Proves that small K yields linear gains (E ≈ K) while larger K plateaus at problem-dependent values Θ(log N), explaining why larger datasets can be repeated more before marginal benefit vanishes.
scaling lawsdata reuselinear regressiontheoretical analysis
ICLR 2026

Sublinear Spectral Clustering Oracle with Little Memory

Problem. Existing sublinear spectral clustering oracles require Omega(sqrt n) memory for data structures, creating bottlenecks for massive graphs and memory-limited settings.
Approach. Establishes memory-time tradeoffs for sublinear spectral clustering oracles on well-clusterable graphs, showing oracles can construct data structures using much smaller than O(sqrt n) memory (e.g. O(n^0.01)) while maintaining sublinear query time.
Contribution. Breaks the Omega(sqrt n) memory barrier and characterizes near-optimal tradeoff frontiers, demonstrating trade-offs like S·T=Õ(n) for logarithmic conductance gap clustering.
spectral clusteringsublinear algorithmsmemory-time tradeoffsgraph algorithms
ICLR 2026

Selection, Reflection and Self-Refinement: Revisit Reasoning Tasks via a Causal Lens

Problem. Reasoning tasks are difficult because their latent space exceeds observation space complexity and latent variables are densely structured with strong dependencies, despite existing models failing to perform reasoning reliably.
Approach. We cast reasoning as a selection mechanism and introduce SR², which incorporates estimated latent variables as feedback with three modules: reflective representation learning, dependency self-refinement, and periodic intermediate alignment.
Contribution. The framework addresses reasoning task challenges by learning dense dependencies among latent representations.
causal selection mechanismreflective representation learningdependency self-refinementintermediate alignment
ICLR 2026

Overlap-weighted orthogonal meta-learner for treatment effect estimation over time

Problem. Estimating heterogeneous treatment effects in time-varying settings is challenged by severe overlap problems—the probability of observing certain treatment sequences decreases exponentially, leaving many sequences under-supported.
Approach. We introduce overlap-weighted orthogonal (WO) meta-learner that targets regions with high probability of receiving interventional treatment sequences, developing a Neyman-orthogonal population risk function.
Contribution. The WO-learner is Neyman-orthogonal (robust to nuisance misspecification), fully model-agnostic, and provides more reliable HTE estimates under low overlap conditions.
overlap weightingorthogonal meta-learningtreatment effect estimationneyman orthogonality
ICLR 2026

Benefits and Limitations of Communication in Multi-Agent Reasoning

Problem. Multi-agent reasoning systems are poorly understood—it is unclear when communication benefits performance and what tradeoffs exist between agent count, communication bandwidth, and problem complexity.
Approach. We propose a theoretical framework analyzing expressivity of multi-agent systems across three algorithmic families (state tracking, recall, k-hop reasoning), deriving bounds on agents required, inter-agent communication, and achievable speedups.
Contribution. Results identify regimes where communication is provably beneficial, delineate tradeoffs between agent count and bandwidth, and expose intrinsic limitations under resource constraints.
multi-agent systems analysiscommunication complexityagent expressivitytheoretical bounds
ICLR 2026

Training-Free Determination of Network Width via Neural Tangent Kernel

Problem. Determining appropriate neural network width under computational constraints is a fundamental challenge without a principled pre-training approach.
Approach. We introduce a practical metric derived from Neural Tangent Kernel (NTK) that estimates minimum network width by examining the smallest eigenvalue of the NTK at initialization.
Contribution. Our NTK-based metric effectively identifies cardinal width—the width at which generalization performance saturates—across multiple datasets and architectures.
neural tangent kerneleigenvalue analysiswidth estimationgeneralization bounds
ICLR 2026

Adversarially Pretrained Transformers may be Universally Robust In-Context Learners

Problem. Adversarial training is computationally expensive, yet its potential as a foundation for universally robust models remains unexplored theoretically.
Approach. Shows that single-layer linear transformers, after adversarial pretraining across diverse tasks, can robustly generalize to unseen tasks through in-context learning.
Contribution. Demonstrates that adversarially pretrained transformers can provide free adversarial robustness to downstream tasks despite expensive initial training.
adversarial trainingin-context learningadversarial robustnessfoundation models
ICLR 2026

Language Identification in the Limit with Computational Trace

Problem. Formal understanding of why training on chain-of-thought computational traces improves LLM capabilities is limited.
Approach. Introduce identification in the limit with trace framework augmenting Gold's paradigm by providing learners with computational traces of accepting machines.
Contribution. Perfect traces make computable languages identifiable in the limit, contrasting sharply with Gold's impossibility result for simpler language classes.
formal language learningcomputational tracesidentification in the limit
ICLR 2026

Price of Quality: Sufficient Conditions for Sparse Recovery using Mixed-Quality Data

Problem. Sparse recovery from mixed-quality data sources requires understanding how to trade off high-quality and low-quality measurements.
Approach. Establish sample-size conditions for information-theoretic and algorithmic sparse recovery; analyze LASSO in agnostic heterogeneous-noise setting.
Contribution. Define Price of Quality trade-off between high and low-quality samples; LASSO threshold matches homogeneous case depending only on average noise.
sparse recoverymixed-quality dataLASSO
ICLR 2026

Covariate-Guided Clusterwise Linear Regression for Generalization to Unseen Data

Problem. Single global linear models fail to capture local relationships that vary across input space regions.
Approach. Covariate-guided CLR jointly learns assignment function and K local regressors via alternating minimization with proxy network.
Contribution. Method spans spectrum from global to instance-wise fits with convergence guarantees and PAC-style excess-risk bounds.
clusterwise linear regressionlocal modelsgradient-based optimization
ICLR 2026

Conformalized Survival Counterfactuals Prediction for General Right-Censored Data

Problem. Existing conformal prediction methods for survival time prediction under right-censoring provide only probably approximately correct (PAC) miscoverage guarantees rather than exact ones.
Approach. A new calibration procedure under the potential outcome framework uses reweighting to transform the problem into weighted conformal inference, enabling lower prediction bound construction via quantile regression with exact miscoverage guarantees and doubly-robust protection.
Contribution. Empirical evaluation on synthetic and real-world clinical data demonstrates the validity and informativeness of the constructed lower prediction bounds.
conformal inferencequantile regressiondoubly robust
ICLR 2026

Stochastic Neural Networks for Causal Inference with Missing Confounders

Problem. Handling missing confounders in observational data is challenging, and existing latent variable approaches suffer from consistency issues in causal effect estimation.
Approach. CI-StoNet uses a stochastic neural network to jointly model outcome functions and missing confounders with an adaptive stochastic gradient SGHMC algorithm, showing that causal effects remain identifiable even when confounders are non-identifiable.
Contribution. CI-StoNet provides state-of-the-art performance on causal effect estimation benchmarks and demonstrates adaptability to proxy variable and multiple-cause scenarios.
stochastic neural networkshamiltonian monte carlocausal inference
ICLR 2026

Feedback-driven recurrent quantum neural network universality

Problem. While quantum reservoir computing shows promise for processing temporal data, the approximation capabilities of feedback-based quantum systems remain theoretically unclear.
Approach. The paper studies approximation capabilities of recurrent quantum neural networks (quantum analogues of classical RNNs) by projecting regular state-space systems into quantum systems with analysis showing no curse of dimensionality.
Contribution. Results show that quantum recurrent neural networks are universal with linear readouts, with qubits growing only logarithmically in the reciprocal of approximation accuracy.
quantum computingreservoir computinguniversal approximation
ICLR 2026

Tight Bounds for Schrodinger Potential Estimation in Unpaired Data Translation

Problem. Estimating Schrodinger potentials for optimal transport between distributions lacks tight sample complexity guarantees when only observing samples from initial and final densities.
Approach. Uses Ornstein-Uhlenbeck reference process with stochastic optimal control to estimate Schrodinger potentials, deriving tight generalization bounds under spectral assumptions via risk minimization.
Contribution. Establishes tight bounds on generalization ability achieving fast convergence rates up to logarithmic factors for Schrodinger potential estimation including Gaussian mixtures.
schrodinger bridgesstochastic optimal controlgeneralization boundsornstein-uhlenbeck process
ICLR 2026

Transformers as Measure-Theoretic Associative Memory: A Statistical Perspective and Minimax Optimality

Problem. Understanding how Transformers learn from long, distributional contexts and recall relevant information remains theoretically unclear despite empirical success in content-addressable retrieval.
Approach. Recasts Transformer attention as measure-theoretic associative memory where context is a distribution and attention is an integral operator, proving softmax attention trained via empirical risk minimization learns recall-and-predict decomposition.
Contribution. Establishes matching minimax lower bounds proving convergence optimality, providing theoretical grounding for Transformer generalization on long distributional contexts.
measure theoryassociative memoryminimax analysisprobability measures
ICLR 2026

Reshaping Reasoning in LLMs: A Theoretical Analysis of RL Training Dynamics through Pattern Selection

Problem. RL training dynamics in LLMs for reasoning remain unclear, with understanding limited to empirical observation of training trajectory.
Approach. Develops theoretical framework via pattern-level and token-level analysis showing RL primarily optimizes sparse critical token subsets, reshaping reasoning pattern distributions under two reward types.
Contribution. Explains RL training dynamics showing verifiable rewards enable convergence to optimal strategies when base reasoning is strong, while internal feedback initially improves then potentially degrades performance.
reinforcement learningreasoning modelspattern analysistraining dynamics
ICLR 2026

Neural Networks Learn Generic Multi-Index Models Near Information-Theoretic Limit

Problem. Understanding how neural networks efficiently learn high-dimensional features in the multi-index model setup remains theoretically open despite empirical success.
Approach. Proves that two-layer network trained via layer-wise gradient descent agnostically learns target with o_d(1) test error using O-tilde(d) samples and O-tilde(d^2) time via power-iteration in first layer.
Contribution. Demonstrates sample and time complexity align with information-theoretic limits up to leading order, proving optimality and that optimal results require first-layer training beyond O(1) steps.
representation learninggradient descentpower iterationmulti-index models
ICLR 2026

Efficient Testing for Correlation Clustering: Improved Algorithms and Optimal Bounds

Problem. Correlation clustering testing requires excessive queries; current state-of-the-art uses Õ(1/ε⁷) queries and time.
Approach. Design improved algorithm testing correlation clustering cost via membership and distance queries with better query complexity bounds.
Contribution. Achieves O(1/ε²) queries and time (improvement from Õ(1/ε⁷)), and tight bounds of Θ(1/ε) for balance problem.
correlation clusteringproperty testingquery complexity
ICLR 2026

ROC-n-reroll: How verifier imperfection affects test-time scaling

Problem. Understanding how verifier imperfection affects test-time scaling methods like Best-of-N and Rejection Sampling remains limited.
Approach. Prove instance-level accuracy is precisely characterized by verifier's ROC curve geometry, providing theoretical understanding of verifier effects.
Contribution. Shows RS outperforms BoN for fixed compute; impossible to predict high-compute performance from low-compute regime observations.
roc curve analysisverifier characterizationtest-time scaling
ICLR 2026

Nonparametric Contextual Online Bilateral Trade

Problem. Online bilateral trade with nonparametric valuations and no valuation observation remains an open problem in contextual learning.
Approach. Characterize general nonparametric setting with arbitrary Lipschitz valuations, design algorithm leveraging contextual information via hierarchical tree construction.
Contribution. Achieve regret Õ(T^((d-1)/d)) under one-bit feedback and strong budget balance with matching lower bounds demonstrating tightness.
contextual banditsbilateral tradehierarchical trees
ICLR 2026

Implicit Regularisation in Diffusion Models: An Algorithm-Dependent Generalisation Analysis

Problem. Generalisation behavior of diffusion models remains poorly understood, especially in high-dimensional settings where they memorize training data without proper regularization.
Approach. A framework of algorithm-dependent generalization leverages score stability (sensitivity of score-matching to dataset perturbations) to derive generalization bounds for diffusion models.
Contribution. Identifies multiple sources of implicit regularization in diffusion models including early stopping, sampler discretisation, and SGD, grounded in algorithmic properties rather than model structure.
generalization theorydiffusion modelsalgorithmic stability
ICLR 2026

JAPAN: Joint Adaptive Prediction Areas with Normalising Flow

Problem. Conformal prediction methods rely on residual-based scores that impose geometric constraints and struggle with multimodal distributions, producing overly conservative prediction areas centered around the mean.
Approach. JAPAN uses normalizing flows to estimate predictive density and constructs prediction areas by thresholding on density scores, enabling compact, potentially disjoint, and context-adaptive regions with finite-sample coverage guarantees.
Contribution. JAPAN demonstrates good calibration and tighter prediction areas compared to existing baselines on multivariate regression and forecasting tasks while retaining finite-sample validity.
normalizing flowsconformal predictiondensity estimationuncertainty quantification
ICLR 2026

Learning Dynamic Causal Graphs Under Parametric Uncertainty via Polynomial Chaos Expansions

Problem. Causal discovery methods assume static graphs, but real-world systems exhibit dynamic causal relationships that vary with underlying parameters, limiting applicability to critical domains like industrial control.
Approach. A novel framework models each causal link as a function of measurable system parameters using Polynomial Chaos Expansions (PCE), enabling tractable learning of functional causal structure from observational data.
Contribution. On a large-scale chemical reactor dataset, the framework learns dynamic causal structure with 90.9% F1-score, nearly doubling baseline performance and providing interpretable models of causal mechanism evolution.
polynomial chaos expansionscausal discoveryparametric causalitydynamic graphs
ICLR 2026

Transformers as Unsupervised Learning Algorithms: A study on Gaussian Mixtures

Problem. Transformers' capabilities in unsupervised learning remain largely unexplored despite their success in supervised in-context learning and other downstream tasks.
Approach. TGMM evaluates transformers on Gaussian Mixture Models (GMMs) and proves that transformers can efficiently approximate both the Expectation-Maximization algorithm and cubic tensor power iterations used in spectral methods.
Contribution. Transformers effectively solve GMMs and mitigate limitations of classical methods like EM while exhibiting reasonable robustness to distribution shifts, providing first theoretical guarantees for high-order tensor operations.
gaussian mixture modelsexpectation-maximizationtensor operationsunsupervised learning
ICLR 2026

The Expressive Limits of Diagonal SSMs for State-Tracking

Problem. Theoretical understanding of the expressive power of diagonal State-Space Models on sequential state-tracking tasks remains limited.
Approach. Studies input-dependent complex-valued diagonal SSMs, proving single-layer can track any Abelian group but not non-Abelian groups, and k-layer models track groups with subnormal series of length k.
Contribution. Establishes tight expressivity bounds for DCD SSMs; empirically reveals gap between theoretical expressivity and practical learnability for non-Abelian groups.
state-space modelsgroup theoryexpressivity analysis
ICLR 2026

Diffusion Language Models are Provably Optimal Parallel Samplers

Problem. Diffusion language models promise faster parallel inference but their theoretical foundations and optimality properties remain unestablished.
Approach. Formalizes a model of parallel sampling and proves DLMs with polynomial-length chain-of-thought can simulate any parallel algorithm optimally, with revision enabling optimal space complexity.
Contribution. Provides theoretical justification for DLMs as efficient samplers and establishes strict expressivity gap showing revision is necessary for optimal performance.
diffusion language modelsparallel samplingchain-of-thought
ICLR 2026

Almost Bayesian: Dynamics of SGD Through Singular Learning Theory

Problem. Understanding how stochastic gradient descent relates to Bayesian sampling in neural networks has been a long-standing open question in deep learning theory.
Approach. Model SGD's late-stage dynamics as diffusion on porous media using singular learning theory, showing how loss-surface degeneracies impact the steady-state distribution.
Contribution. SGD's local steady-state distribution is effectively a tempered Bayesian posterior accounting for local accessibility constraints.
singular learning theorydiffusion modelingbayesian inference
ICLR 2026

Heterogeneous Front-Door Effects: Debiased Estimation with Quasi-Oracle Guarantees

Problem. Front-door adjustment identifies causal effects under unmeasured confounding via mediators, but existing work focuses on average treatment effects rather than heterogeneous effects.
Approach. Develop debiased learners (FD-DR-Learner, FD-R-Learner) for heterogeneous front-door effects that attain quasi-oracle rates even when nuisance functions converge slowly.
Contribution. Both learners achieve fast quasi-oracle convergence rates with robust, debiased heterogeneous treatment effect estimates under front-door scenarios.
debiased learningheterogeneous treatment effectsfront-door adjustment
ICLR 2026

Does Weak-to-strong Generalization Happen under Spurious Correlations?

Problem. Weak-to-strong generalization under spurious correlations remains unclear: when pseudolabels from weak teacher on group-imbalanced data are used for fine-tuning.
Approach. Theoretical analysis shows W2S always succeeds when minority fractions match (η_u=η_ℓ) but fails when mismatched, with gain diminishing as (η_u-η_ℓ)² increases.
Contribution. W2S gain depends on minority-group fraction alignment; simple retraining on high-confidence data subset achieves consistent improvements without group labels.
weak-to-strong generalizationspurious correlationsconfidence-based retraining
ICLR 2026

Laplacian Kernelized Bandit

Problem. Multi-user contextual bandits where users relate via graphs and exhibit non-linear rewards with graph homophily require principled exploration strategies.
Approach. Introduce multi-user RKHS combining graph Laplacian with base arm kernel; design GP-based algorithms (LK-GP-UCB, LK-GP-TS) for this unified kernel.
Contribution. Regret bounds scale with effective dimension of multi-user kernel, not user count; outperforms baselines in non-linear settings.
laplacian kernelsgraph-based banditsgaussian processes
ICLR 2026

An evolutionary perspective on modes of learning in Transformers

Problem. Transformers use in-weight learning (parameter refinement) and in-context learning (activation modulation); when each strategy is preferred under different conditions is unclear.
Approach. Use evolutionary biology framework operationalizing environmental stability and cue reliability in controlled tasks (sinusoid regression, Omniglot).
Contribution. Stable environments favor IWL with sharp transitions; reliable cues favor ICL in volatile settings; task structure governs strategy transitions.
in-context learningin-weight learningevolutionary framework
ICLR 2026

The Price of Robustness: Stable Classifiers Need Overparameterization

Problem. Overparameterization, stability, and generalization relationships remain unclear for discontinuous classifiers.
Approach. Establish generalization bound for finite classes improving inversely with class stability (expected margin distance); derive law of robustness.
Contribution. Interpolating models with p≈n parameters on n points must be unstable; substantial overparameterization necessary for high stability.
generalization boundsstability analysisrobustness theory
ICLR 2026

Learning to Answer from Correct Demonstrations

Problem. Learning from demonstrations with multiple correct answers typically assumes the demonstrator belongs to a low-complexity policy class, which may not hold in practice.
Approach. The paper proposes a reward-model-centric approach assuming low cardinality of the reward class rather than the policy class, and develops a sample-efficient learning method with logarithmic sample complexity in reward cardinality.
Contribution. The novel approach achieves logarithmic sample complexity in reward class cardinality, outperforming likelihood maximization methods in settings with multiple correct demonstrations.
imitation learningcontextual banditsreward modeling
ICLR 2026

A Recovery Guarantee for Sparse Neural Networks

Problem. Sparse recovery for neural networks lacks theoretical guarantees, limiting understanding of when and how sparse networks can be recovered.
Approach. The paper proves recovery guarantees for ReLU networks where iterative hard thresholding recovers sparse weights exactly using memory linear in the number of nonzero weights.
Contribution. First recovery guarantees for sparse neural networks on synthetic planted MLPs, MNIST classification, and implicit neural representations, with experimental performance competitive or exceeding prior methods.
sparse recoveryiterative hard thresholdingrecovery guarantees
ICLR 2026

Online Pseudo-Zeroth-Order Training of Neuromorphic Spiking Neural Networks

Problem. Training deep SNNs in a biologically plausible way remains challenging because spatial backpropagation violates neuromorphic properties and requires symmetric weights.
Approach. Online Pseudo-Zeroth-Order (OPZO) training requires only single forward propagation with noise injection and direct top-down signals for spatial credit assignment, enabling on-chip training.
Contribution. OPZO achieves competitive performance with spatial BP while avoiding symmetric weights and separate forward-backward phases, applicable to both fully connected and convolutional SNNs.
zeroth-order trainingspiking neural networksneuromorphic computing
ICLR 2026

Better Bounds for the Distributed Experts Problem

Problem. Distributed experts problem requires minimizing regret across networked servers while controlling communication overhead, with prior work using suboptimal protocols.
Approach. Proposes a protocol that achieves low regret by computing ell_p norm losses aggregated across servers and optimizing communication-regret tradeoff.
Contribution. Achieves regret roughly 1/(sqrt(T)*polylog(nsT)) with O(n/R^2 + s/R^2)*max(s^(1-2/p),1)*polylog(nsT) bits of communication, improving prior work.
distributed learningexpert aggregationcommunication complexity
ICLR 2026

When Bias Helps Learning: Bridging Initial Prejudice and Trainability

Problem. Random network initialization exhibits initial-guessing bias toward specific classes, and prior mean-field analyses fail to link this to trainability.
Approach. Provides theoretical proof linking initial-guessing bias to mean-field analyses, showing that optimally trainable initialization is systematically biased rather than neutral.
Contribution. Connects network initialization bias to learning efficiency, establishing counterintuitive finding that biased initialization optimizes trainability.
mean-field analysisinitialization biastrainability
ICLR 2026

Dimension-Free Minimax Rates for Learning Pairwise Interactions in Attention-Style Models

Problem. Understanding the sample complexity of learning pairwise interactions in attention-style models remains unclear, particularly how dimension and model structure affect convergence rates.
Approach. Provides theoretical analysis of minimax convergence rates for single-layer attention models showing dependence only on activation smoothness, independent of token count and ambient dimension.
Contribution. Proves dimension-free minimax rate of M^(-2β/(2β+1)) that is independent of token count, ambient dimension, or weight matrix rank, establishing fundamental efficiency of attention mechanisms.
minimax analysisattention mechanismssample complexity
ICLR 2026

Critical attention scaling in long-context transformers

Problem. As context length increases in transformers, attention scores collapse toward uniformity causing tokens to cluster excessively, degrading model capability.
Approach. Analyzes attention scaling through a simplified tractable model showing phase transition governed by scaling factor βn, identifying critical scaling of βn ≈ log n.
Contribution. Provides rigorous theoretical justification for logarithmic attention scaling used in YaRN and Qwen, explaining how it maintains sparse content-adaptive attention.
attention mechanismsphase transitionscontext length scaling
ICLR 2026

Efficient and Sharp Off-Policy Learning under Unobserved Confounding

Problem. Standard policy learning assumes unconfoundedness, producing biased estimates when unobserved confounding exists, potentially leading to harmful policies.
Approach. Employ causal sensitivity analysis to derive a semi-parametrically efficient estimator for sharp bounds on value functions under unobserved confounding, avoiding unstable inverse propensity weighting.
Contribution. Achieves optimal confounding-robust policy selection with applications to healthcare and public policy, outperforming plug-in approaches.
causal sensitivity analysisrobustnesssemi-parametric efficiencypolicy improvement
ICLR 2026

Differentially Private Equilibrium Finding in Polymatrix Games

Problem. Achieving both high-accuracy equilibria and low privacy budgets in polymatrix game equilibrium finding is fundamentally limited under differential privacy constraints.
Approach. Establish hardness results showing impossibility of simultaneous accuracy and vanishing privacy budget, propose distributed algorithm leveraging polymatrix structure properties.
Contribution. First algorithm achieving vanishing Nash gap and privacy budget simultaneously as players increase, with numerical validation.
differential privacygame theoryequilibrium computationdistributed learning
ICLR 2026

No Prior, No Leakage: Revisiting Reconstruction Attacks in Trained Neural Networks

Problem. Training set reconstruction attacks from model parameters are poorly understood theoretically and fail without prior knowledge about data distribution.
Approach. Rigorously prove that without data priors, infinitely many alternative solutions exist arbitrarily far from the true training set, making reconstruction fundamentally unreliable.
Contribution. Shows networks trained more extensively with stronger implicit bias are less susceptible to reconstruction attacks, reconciling privacy with generalization.
privacyreconstruction attackstheoretical analysisimplicit bias
ICLR 2026

Time-To-Inconsistency: A Survival Analysis of Large Language Model Robustness to Adversarial Attacks

Problem. LLM robustness in extended multi-turn conversations remains poorly understood; existing benchmarks focus on static single-turn assessments that miss real-world temporal degradation patterns.
Approach. Applies survival analysis models (Cox proportional hazards, Accelerated Failure Time, Random Survival Forest) to 36,951 dialogue turns to model conversational failure as a time-to-event process with semantic drift features.
Contribution. Establishes survival analysis as a paradigm for evaluating multi-turn robustness; turn-level risk monitors flag failing conversations several turns before the first inconsistency while maintaining modest false-alert rates.
survival analysiscox modelsemantic drifttime-to-event modeling
ICLR 2026

Graphon Cross-Validation: Assessing Models on Network Data

Problem. Graphon models for complex networks lack accurate parameter selection methods; tuning parameters that govern smoothness is critical but computationally expensive.
Approach. Proposes a novel graphon cross-validation method for selecting tuning parameters and estimation approaches; asymptotically parallel to estimation error.
Contribution. Cross-validation score is theoretically sound and computationally efficient; consistently delivers superior accuracy across simulations and real-world applications.
cross-validationgraphon modelsparameter selectionnetwork estimation
ICLR 2026

Why Less is More (Sometimes): A Theory of Data Curation

Problem. Classical scaling laws suggest more data improves performance, but recent methods achieve better results with smaller curated datasets; when and why less is more remains unclear.
Approach. Theoretical framework modeling data curation with an imperfect oracle selecting examples by difficulty and correctness; derives exact scaling law curves revealing phase transitions for when curation improves generalization.
Contribution. Provides analytical conditions and exact phase transition curves; validates on ImageNet that curated datasets can outperform full datasets and mitigate model collapse.
scaling lawsdata curationphase transitionstheoretical analysis
ICLR 2026

GenSR: Symbolic regression based on equation generative space

Problem. Discrete equation space search in symbolic regression produces noisy fitting feedback that poorly guides exploration toward better equations.
Approach. GenSR maps equations into a generative latent space using a Conditional Variational Autoencoder with symbolic continuity and local numerical smoothness, then coarsely localizes and refines candidates via CMA-ES.
Contribution. GenSR jointly optimizes predictive accuracy, expression simplicity, and computational efficiency while remaining robust to noise.
variational autoencodercma-essymbolic regression
ICLR 2026

Learning to Play Multi-Follower Bayesian Stackelberg Games

Problem. Online learning in multi-follower Bayesian Stackelberg games with unknown type distributions requires algorithms with low regret despite the exponential complexity.
Approach. The authors design learning algorithms under different information settings, achieving regret bounds that do not grow polynomially with the number of followers using type feedback or action feedback.
Contribution. The work provides nearly tight regret bounds showing sublinear dependence on the number of followers under type feedback and discusses lower bounds for the action feedback case.
stackelberg gamesregret minimizationonline learning
ICLR 2026

The Logical Expressiveness of Topological Neural Networks

Problem. The logical expressiveness and representational limitations of topological neural networks remain uncharacterized.
Approach. The authors analyze isomorphism tests from topological neural networks, introduce k-CCWL tests for combinatorial complexes, and propose topological counting logic TC_k with pairwise counting quantifiers.
Contribution. The study proves exact equivalence between k-CCWL, TC_k, and topological pebble games, establishing a logical expressiveness theory for TNNs.
topological neural networkscounting logicweisfeiler-lehman
ICLR 2026

Learning a Game by Paying the Agents

Problem. Learning utility functions of no-regret learning agents in repeated games requires methods that can interact with agents through signals and payments.
Approach. The principal formulates a zero-sum game where payment functions are the principal's strategy space and uses this framework to learn utilities.
Contribution. The principal can learn utility functions to any desired precision using a polynomial number of rounds and can then steer agents to desired equilibria.
game theoryutility learningno-regret learning
ICLR 2026

Unlearning Evaluation through Subset Statistical Independence

Problem. Evaluating machine unlearning is challenging because existing methods require retraining reference models or membership inference attacks, both impractical without prior access to training configuration.
Approach. The paper proposes a subset-level evaluation framework using Hilbert-Schmidt Independence Criterion to assess whether model outputs on a subset exhibit statistical dependence.
Contribution. The method provides a simple, standalone evaluation procedure that reliably distinguishes in-training from out-of-training subsets and differentiates unlearning effectiveness.
subset statistical independencehilbert-schmidt criterionunlearning evaluation
ICLR 2026

Preventing Model Collapse Under Overparametrization: Optimal Mixing Ratios for Interpolation Learning and Ridge Regression

Problem. Model collapse occurs when generative models degrade after repeatedly training on synthetic outputs, limiting the scalability of self-improving systems.
Approach. The paper analyzes optimal mixing ratios in overparameterized linear regression combining fresh real labels with synthetic labels, deriving generalization error formulae for interpolation and ridge regression.
Contribution. Proves optimal real-data proportion converges to reciprocal of golden ratio for min-l2-norm interpolation, with optimal mixing ratios of at least one-half across covariate distributions.
generalization error analysisoptimal mixing weightssynthetic data composition
ICLR 2026

Oracle-efficient Hybrid Learning with Constrained Adversaries

Problem. Hybrid online learning (i.i.d. features, adversarial labels) shows dichotomy between statistically-optimal but intractable algorithms and computationally-efficient but suboptimal ones.
Approach. For structured settings where adversaries are constrained to fixed label function class, an oracle-efficient algorithm achieves regret scaling with derived Rademacher complexity.
Contribution. This algorithm enables simultaneous statistical optimality and computational efficiency with applications to equilibrium computation in high-dimensional stochastic zero-sum games.
frank-wolfe reductionrademacher complexityoracle-efficient learning
ICLR 2026

The Coverage Principle: How Pre-Training Enables Post-Training

Problem. Pre-training success quantified by cross entropy is poorly predictive of downstream performance, yet how pre-training shapes final model success remains poorly understood.
Approach. Theoretical analysis through the lens of coverage—probability mass on high-quality responses—which is necessary and sufficient for post-training and test-time scaling.
Contribution. The coverage principle reveals next-token prediction implicitly optimizes toward good coverage, which generalizes faster than cross entropy, with practical interventions for model selection and decoding.
coverage principletest-time scalinggradient normalization
ICLR 2026

Fast Escape, Slow Convergence: Learning Dynamics of Phase Retrieval under Power-Law Data

Problem. Scaling laws in nonlinear models with anisotropic data are not well understood, particularly how spectral decay affects convergence dynamics.
Approach. Develops a tractable reduction revealing three-phase trajectory: fast escape, slow convergence, and spectral-tail learning, deriving explicit scaling laws.
Contribution. Provides first rigorous characterization of scaling laws in nonlinear regression with anisotropic power-law data.
phase retrievalhigh-dimensional analysisspectral decomposition
ICLR 2026

High-Dimensional Analysis of Single-Layer Attention for Sparse-Token Classification

Problem. How can attention mechanisms learn to selectively attend to weak signals embedded in random subsets of tokens in long sequences?
Approach. Studies single-layer attention in a sparse-token classification model theoretically, proving query weights acquire signal alignment after two gradient updates and deriving exact asymptotic test error.
Contribution. Shows attention enables logarithmic signal strength scaling (vs. linear for classifiers) and provides exact asymptotic error expression quantifying capacity.
attention mechanismsparse-token classificationhigh-dimensional analysis
ICLR 2026

Saddle-To-Saddle Dynamics in Deep ReLU Networks: Low-Rank Bias in the First Saddle Escape

Problem. When deep ReLU networks are initialized with small weights, gradient descent escapes a saddle point at the origin, but the escape dynamics and structure are poorly understood.
Approach. The paper analyzes escape directions and their properties, discovering that deeper layers exhibit a low-rank bias where the first singular value is at least ℓ^(1/4) larger than others.
Contribution. Reveals that deep ReLU networks exhibit saddle-to-saddle dynamics with GD visiting a sequence of saddles with increasing bottleneck rank.
gradient descentsaddle point analysislow-rank structure
ICLR 2026

Tractability via Low Dimensionality: The Parameterized Complexity of Training Quantized Neural Networks

Problem. Neural network training complexity is understudied in quantized settings despite quantization's practical benefits for speed and energy efficiency.
Approach. Initiates complexity-theoretic study of ReLU network training under full quantization with fixed-parameter tractable cases parameterized by input dimensionality, width, output dimensionality or error bound.
Contribution. Establishes strong lower bounds showing hardness in binary and restrictive settings, while identifying tractable cases via input dimensionality combined with width and output parameters.
quantizationneural network trainingparameterized complexity
ICLR 2026

Identifiability Challenges in Sparse Linear Ordinary Differential Equations

Problem. Identifiability of sparse linear ODEs from data is poorly characterized despite its practical relevance and contrast with the dense case.
Approach. Characterizes identifiability of sparse linear ODEs, showing unidentifiability occurs with positive probability in practically relevant sparsity regimes.
Contribution. Provides lower bounds for unidentifiability probability and empirically corroborates theoretical unidentifiability persists in practice despite inductive biases.
dynamical systemsidentifiabilitysparse models
ICLR 2026

InfoNCE Induces Gaussian Distribution

Problem. Contrastive learning produces embeddings with unclear distributional properties, making it difficult to derive analytical expressions for likelihood, entropy, and mutual information.
Approach. Analysis shows that InfoNCE-trained embeddings are well-approximated by multivariate Gaussian distributions under alignment and concentration assumptions; added regularization promotes low norm and high entropy.
Contribution. Features from InfoNCE training follow near-precise Gaussian distributions, enabling analytic expressions in the representation space and applications in likelihood-based inference.
contrastive learninggaussian approximationhigh-dimensional analysis
ICLR 2026

ON THE ROLE OF IMPLICIT REGULARIZATION OF STOCHASTIC GRADIENT DESCENT IN GROUP ROBUSTNESS

Problem. Training with stochastic gradient descent at moderately large learning rates improves robustness against spurious correlations, but the underlying mechanism remains unclear.
Approach. The work identifies batch size as critical and shows robustness gains arise from implicit regularization of SGD intensifying with larger learning rates and smaller batch sizes.
Contribution. Theoretically establishes the phenomenon in linear models and empirically demonstrates SGD systematically suppresses spurious feature dependence across multiple deep neural network benchmarks.
stochastic gradient descentimplicit regularizationspurious correlations
ICLR 2026

How reinforcement learning after next-token prediction facilitates learning

Problem. Reinforcement learning after next-token prediction works well in reasoning domains, but the optimization mechanisms enabling this success are not theoretically understood.
Approach. They study a theoretical framework where autoregressive transformers are first trained on next-token prediction over a mixture of short and long chain-of-thought sequences, then fine-tuned with RL. For the parity prediction task, they prove RL enables generalization when the proportion of long demonstrations is not exponentially small.
Contribution. They expose optimization mechanisms by which RL improves over next-token prediction, showing RL leverages longer responses to facilitate learning and deriving theoretical bounds for linear models on parity tasks.
reinforcement learningnext-token predictionchain-of-thoughtautoregressive transformers
ICLR 2026

ActiveCQ: Active Estimation of Causal Quantities

Problem. Estimating causal quantities typically requires large datasets, which can be expensive when measuring outcomes is costly, and prior work focuses narrowly on conditional average treatment effects.
Approach. ActiveCQ formalizes a broader task of actively estimating causal quantities and proposes a unified framework using Gaussian Processes for regression with conditional mean embeddings for the distribution component, enabling principled acquisition strategies.
Contribution. Their framework significantly outperforms baselines, achieving substantial gains in sample efficiency across a variety of causal quantities through information gain and total variance reduction strategies.

On the Convergence Behavior of Preconditioned Gradient Descent Toward the Rich Learning Regime

Problem. Spectral bias causes neural networks to learn low frequencies first, enhancing generalization but limiting fine-scale structure learning, and grokking delays training through gradual transitions between learning regimes.
Approach. They explore how preconditioned gradient descent (PGD) like Gauss-Newton mitigates spectral bias and grokking by enabling uniform parameter space exploration in the NTK regime without spectral bias impediment.
Contribution. They provide evidence that grokking represents a transitional behavior between the lazy NTK regime and the rich regime, demonstrating how PGD reduces these transition delays.

Problem. Existing theoretical analyses of transformers assume idealized settings like infinite data or orthogonal embeddings, but realistic settings use finite data with non-orthogonal random embeddings.
Approach. They analyze a single-layer transformer with random embeddings trained on a token-retrieval task, tracking early-phase gradient descent to derive explicit storage capacity formulas revealing multiplicative dependence between sample size, embedding dimension, and sequence length.
Contribution. They provide explicit formulas for model storage capacity and lower bounds demonstrating that multiplicative scaling is inherent under non-orthogonal embeddings.

Problem. Learning in decentralized matching markets with adversarial corruptions of reward feedback undermines convergence to optimal matches.
Approach. For known corruption levels, they develop a robust Explore-Then-Gale-Shapley variant with widened confidence intervals; for unknown corruption, they propose Multi-layer ETGS race adaptively mitigating adversarial effects.
Contribution. Both algorithms provide theoretical guarantees with upper bounds on optimal stable regret and lower bounds demonstrating optimality.

The Softmax Bottleneck Does Not Limit the Probabilities of the Most Likely Tokens

Problem. The softmax bottleneck in transformer output projections is hypothesized to limit LLMs' ability to express accurate next-token probabilities aligned with natural language statistics.
Approach. Theoretical analysis provides bounds showing that randomly initialized projection matrices can produce accurate probabilities for the top-m tokens, with empirical validation on random and trained matrices.
Contribution. The softmax bottleneck does not significantly limit LLM capabilities for expressing accurate top-token probabilities, with derived bounds on maximal m values.

Intrinsic training dynamics of deep neural networks

Problem. Understanding whether gradient-based training promotes parameters belonging to lower-dimensional structures remains a fundamental unsolved challenge in deep learning theory.
Approach. The paper studies when gradient flow on a parameter θ implies an intrinsic gradient flow on a lifted variable z=φ(θ), establishing a criterion based on kernel inclusion of linear maps and applying it to ReLU networks with path-lifting and linear networks with weight-matrix products.
Contribution. For dense sets of initializations, the flow can be rewritten as an intrinsic dynamic in lower dimensions; the analysis shows relaxed balanced initializations ensure the intrinsic metric property for linear networks.

The Serial Scaling Hypothesis

Problem. Machine learning focuses on parallelization but some problems are fundamentally sequential, like mathematical reasoning and physical simulations, creating a critical blind spot in model design.
Approach. Formalizes the notion of inherently serial problems in complexity theory and demonstrates that current parallel-centric architectures face fundamental limitations on such tasks.
Contribution. Shows for the first time that diffusion models are incapable of solving inherently serial problems despite their sequential nature, with implications for model design and hardware development.
complexity theorydiffusion modelssequential computation
ICLR 2026

Random Spiking Neural Networks are Stable and Spectrally Simple

Problem. Spiking neural networks lack theoretical foundations regarding stability and robustness compared to artificial neural networks, limiting their practical deployment.
Approach. Analyzes discrete-time leaky integrate-and-fire SNNs through Boolean function analysis, focusing on noise sensitivity and stability in classification tasks using Fourier spectrum concentration.
Contribution. Shows that wide LIF-SNN classifiers are stable on average due to concentration of Fourier spectrum on low-frequency components, with experiments confirming stability properties persist in trained networks.
spiking neural networksfourier analysisboolean function analysis

Transfer Learning in Infinite Width Feature Learning Networks

Problem. Existing transfer learning theory for neural networks provides limited understanding of when pretraining improves downstream generalization, especially in feature learning regimes.
Approach. Develops theory for infinite-width networks under gradient flow, analyzing both fine-tuning on source-induced features and jointly rich settings where downstream operates in feature learning regime.
Contribution. Provides interpretable conclusions on transfer performance depending on data regimes, task alignment, and feature learning strength, validated on linear/polynomial regression and real datasets.

Approach. Proposes Personalized Collaborative Learning framework with affinity-based variance reduction, using bias and importance correction mechanisms to handle environment and objective heterogeneity.

Approach. Develops minimax analysis of ReLU message-passing GNNs with explicit architectural assumptions in both inductive and transductive settings.

Subquadratic Algorithms and Hardness for Attention with Any Temperature

Problem. Standard transformer attention has quadratic time complexity in context length; existing subquadratic methods require unrealistic assumptions on entry size or temperature.
Approach. For any constant head dimension, the paper gives the first subquadratic O(n^{2-1/d} · polylog(B)) algorithm for large entry sizes B, with hardness results under SETH.
Contribution. Subquadratic attention algorithms holding for arbitrary temperatures and large head dimensions, plus hardness characterization of when fast attention is possible.

Implicit Models: Expressive Power Scales with Test-Time Compute

Approach. Non-parametric analysis shows that implicit operators through iteration progressively express more complex mappings; expressive power grows with test-time compute, ultimately matching richer function classes.

Approach. Analysis through the Weisfeiler-Leman test shows that the full WL hierarchy cannot distinguish between satisfiable and unsatisfiable instances in general, and studies expressivity needs for various SAT instance families.
Contribution. Results reveal that while random instances are largely distinguishable, industrial instances often require more expressivity than WL-bounded solvers can provide.

A Law of Data Reconstruction for Random Features (And Beyond)

Problem. Deep learning models memorize training data, but prior theory frames memorization as interpolation while overlooking that data reconstruction requires parameter count to exceed data dimensionality times sample count.
Approach. Analysis of the random features model demonstrates that when p >> dn (p=parameters, d=data dimensionality, n=samples), training data can be reconstructed from model parameters.
Contribution. A law of data reconstruction reveals the threshold p=dn beyond which the training dataset can be recovered, with an optimization method demonstrated across various architectures.

Contribution. Reveals that token-wise prediction errors accumulate over time and can cause generalization collapse if length is unconstrained; provides theoretical guarantees for ICL generalization.

Problem. Feature bagging ensemble strategies lack formal understanding of how they improve generalization, and their relationship to instance-based stability concepts remains unclear.
Approach. Analyzes feature bagging through algorithmic stability by introducing feature instability (FI), proving that feature bagging improves stability relative to non-bagged counterparts with larger improvements under aggressive subsampling.
Contribution. Establishes formal guarantees for feature bagging in parametric linear and model-free settings, showing modest bagging rounds suffice to approach infinite-bagging stability levels.

Contribution. Yields the first tight, sharp characterization of optimal sequential testing procedures under Markovian dependence with applications to model misspecification detection and testing structural properties.

Approach. The paper establishes a provable exponential separation by analyzing the quantum algorithm for solving well-conditioned linear systems with sparse rows and columns, proving quantum-inspired classical methods cannot match.
Contribution. First provable exponential separation between quantum and quantum-inspired classical algorithms for a natural machine learning task (linear systems with sparse structure).

Emergent Alignment via Competition

Problem. Aligning AI systems with human values remains a fundamental challenge, but does our inability to create perfectly aligned models preclude obtaining the benefits of alignment? We study a strategic setting where a human user interacts with multiple differently misaligned AI agents, none of which are individually well-aligned.
Approach. Our key insight is that when the user’s utility lies approximately within the convex hull of the agents’ utilities, a condition that becomes easier to satisfy as model diversity increases, strategic competition can yield outcomes comparable to interacting with a perfectly aligned model.
Contribution. We model this as a multi-leader Stackelberg game, extending Bayesian persuasion to multi-round conversations between differently informed parties, and prove three results: (1) when perfect alignment would allow the user to learn her Bayes-optimal action, she can also do so in all equilibria under the convex hull condition; (2) under weaker assumptions requiring only approximate utility learning, a non-strategic user employing quantal response achieves near-optimal utility in all equilibria; and (3) when the user selects the best single AI after an evaluation period, equilibrium guarantees remain near-optimal without further distributional assumptions. We complement the theory with two forms of empirical evidence: First, we perform simulations of the best-AI selection game using best response dynamics, which show that competition among individually misaligned agents reliably improves user utility when the approximate convex hull assumption is satisfied, but does not alw.
neural network

Contribution. Calibrated PFNs produce ATE uncertainty that asymptotically matches frequentist uncertainty and is well-calibrated in finite samples.
prior-data fitted networksposterior calibrationmartingale posteriors

On the Effect of Misspecifying the Embedding Dimension in Low-rank Network Models

Problem. Choosing the correct embedding dimension for network models is difficult, and little is known about behavior when dimension is misspecified.
Approach. Analyzes dimension misspecification under random dot product graphs using random matrix theory, showing oversizing slows convergence while undersizing creates fundamental error floors.
Contribution. Establishes theoretical bounds on estimation error under both over- and under-specified dimensions, with synthetic experiments confirming theoretical predictions.
random matrix theoryspectral analysisnetwork modelsdimension selection
ICML 2026

Inference-time Alignment with Rewards in Besov Spaces: Provable Advantages of Feature Learning and Multi-Step Policy Updates

Problem. Inference-time alignment via reward feedback lacks theoretical foundations, especially for practical settings using neural network reward models.
Approach. Assumes true reward lies in Besov spaces capturing non-uniform smoothness, comparing neural networks to linear estimators and analyzing multi-step alternating algorithms.
Contribution. Shows feature learning capability of neural networks is crucial for improving performance, with multi-step algorithms improving regret especially under local structure.
besov spacesfeature learningmulti-step optimizationreward estimation
ICML 2026

Any-dimensional invariant universality

Problem. Any-dimensional models accepting inputs of varying sizes lack clear universality definitions since traditional universality applies only to fixed-size inputs.
Approach. Develops systematic approach identifying any-dimensional functions with unique functions in infinite-dimensional limit spaces, using symmetries and input relations.
Contribution. Establishes any-dimensional universality framework, shows existing architectures fail universality, and proposes modifications restoring it.
universality theoryinfinite-dimensional spacessymmetry analysisarchitecture design
ICML 2026

Tightening the Score Matching Gap for Diffusion Models

Problem. The score matching gap in diffusion models relates KL divergence to score matching loss but remains loose and non-descriptive of actual sample quality.
Approach. Provides theoretical analysis developing tighter bounds for KL, reverse KL, and Wasserstein distance by exploiting regularity of score estimators and contraction properties.
Contribution. Shows score approximation quality has more impact on closing the gap for low noise scales, using entropy flows and Sobolev inequalities.
score matchingentropy flowslogarithmic sobolev inequalitiesdiffusion analysis
ICML 2026

Utility Boundary of Dataset Distillation: Scaling and Coverage Laws

Problem. Dataset distillation theory lacks quantitative boundaries explaining why heterogeneous matching objectives work and when robustness breaks under configuration changes.
Approach. Proposes configuration-dynamics-error analysis treating training configurations as update operators, measuring robustness via test-risk gap between distilled and full data.
Contribution. Establishes two predictive laws: gap decays as O(k^-1/2) until configuration floor, and required k grows linearly with configuration diversity.
configuration dynamicsdistillation theorycovering numbersrisk bounds
ICML 2026

Dichotomy of Feature Learning and Unlearning: Fast-Slow Analysis on Neural Networks with Stochastic Gradient Descent

Problem. Neural network training dynamics exhibit complex behaviors including feature unlearning, where models progressively lose previously learned features over long training, but the mechanisms driving this remain unclear.
Approach. The paper analyzes infinite-width two-layer networks with large-batch stochastic gradient descent using fast-slow dynamics, deriving differential equations where rapid first-layer weight alignment interacts with slow second-layer development to determine whether feature unlearning occurs.
Contribution. The analysis reveals that the strength of primary nonlinear terms in data induces feature unlearning while initial second-layer weight scales can mitigate it, with validated scaling laws and insights grounded in tensor programs and singular perturbation theory.
fast-slow dynamicstensor programssingular perturbation theory
ICML 2026

A Solvable High-Dimensional Model Where Nonlinear Autoencoders Learn Structure Invisible to PCA While Test Loss Misaligns With Generalization

Problem. Real-world datasets contain hidden structure in higher-order moments invisible to linear methods like PCA, but analyzing when and why nonlinear autoencoders extract this structure remains theoretically intractable.
Approach. The paper introduces a spiked model with two latent factors: one visible to covariance and one uncorrelated yet statistically dependent, proving that nonlinear autoencoders extract both while PCA and linear methods miss the latter.
Contribution. The model demonstrates a tractable case where self-supervised test loss misaligns with representation quality—nonlinear autoencoders recover latent structure that linear methods miss despite higher reconstruction loss.
spiked covariance modelnonlinear autoencoder analysisself-supervised learning theory
ICML 2026

Improved Distribution Estimation in $\ell_\infty$

Problem. Estimating discrete probability distributions under the ℓ∞ norm lacks tight minimax bounds and does not resolve open theoretical questions.
Approach. Derives improved minimax bounds in expectation and high-probability tail bounds, fully empirical versions of the tightest risk bounds, and identifies worst-case extremal distributions.
Contribution. Resolves open questions from Kontorovich & Painsky (2025) regarding ℓ∞ distribution estimation; provides both theoretical bounds and empirical validation.
minimax analysisempirical risk boundsdistribution estimation
ICML 2026

Distortion of AI Alignment Revisited: RLHF is a Decent Utilitarian Aligner

Problem. RLHF's distortion (gap between RLHF policy utility and optimal) can scale exponentially with preference heterogeneity, questioned in pluralistic settings.
Approach. Analyzes RLHF with reward clipping, proving distortion depends on distribution mismatch between preference data and KL reference policy rather than being fundamental to the algorithm.
Contribution. Establishes tight bounds on RLHF distortion (Θ(βB + β) in representative regimes); shows RLHF achieves optimal O(β) distortion when μ = π_ref, suggesting on-policy data improves pluralistic alignment.
rlhf analysisdistortion boundsdistribution mismatch
ICML 2026

How Many Different Outputs Can a Transformer Generate?

Problem. Understanding how many distinct sequences a transformer can output requires predicting expressiveness from limited architectural characteristics.
Approach. Provides upper bounds on accessible sequences depending on prompt length and proves three properties: linear growth of max accessible sequence length, exponential decay beyond threshold, and theoretical upper bound on linear coefficient.
Contribution. Empirically tight bounds (within factor <10 across architectures); explains transformer failures on simple tasks like copying; shows exponential decay holds even with unbounded context.
expressiveness analysissequence enumerationarchitectural limits
ICML 2026

Rank-Learner: Orthogonal Ranking of Treatment Effects

Problem. Ranking individuals by treatment effects differs from estimating exact effect magnitudes, but direct ranking learning remains largely unexplored.
Approach. Rank-Learner is a two-stage learner optimizing a pairwise objective that recovers true treatment effect ordering via Neyman-orthogonal design, avoiding explicit CATE estimation.
Contribution. Consistently outperforms standard CATE estimators and non-orthogonal ranking methods while providing theoretical guarantees including robustness to nuisance function estimation errors.
causal inferenceorthogonal estimationpairwise ranking
ICML 2026

A theory of learning data statistics in diffusion models, from easy to hard

Problem. Diffusion model learning dynamics remain poorly understood, particularly how models learn simple versus complex data statistics.
Approach. Use the mixed cumulant model to control pair-wise and higher-order correlations, proving that denoisers learn pair-wise statistics at linear sample complexity while higher-order statistics require cubic complexity.
Contribution. Introduces the diffusion information exponent as a scalar invariant governing sample complexity of learning different order correlations in diffusion models.
diffusion modelscumulant analysissample complexity
ICML 2026

The Cost of Learning Under Multiple Change Points

Problem. Online learning in environments with multiple change points exhibits endogenous confounding causing catastrophic failure in classical detection schemes.
Approach. Anytime Tracking CUSUM (ATC) is a horizon-free algorithm implementing selective detection that balances ignoring small shifts while reacting quickly to significant ones.
Contribution. Achieves nearly minimax-optimal regret guaranteed to closely match novel information-theoretic lower bound for multiple change point learning.
change point detectiononline learningcusum algorithms
ICML 2026

Statistical Consistency and Generalization of Contrastive Representation Learning

Problem. Theoretical understanding of contrastive representation learning is incomplete: statistical consistency remains unclear, generalization bounds deteriorate with negative samples, and retrieval performance lacks analysis.
Approach. Develop unified statistical learning theory using AUC-type population criterion for downstream tasks and deriving generalization bounds showing trade-off between negative samples and anchor points.
Contribution. Derives bounds O(1/m + 1/√n) and O(1/√m + 1/√n) explaining empirical advantages of large negative sets; extensive experiments on vision-language models validate predictions.
contrastive learningstatistical theorygeneralization bounds
ICML 2026

Power-Boosted Granger-Causal Discovery for Large Heterogeneous Panel Data

Problem. Granger causality testing in high-dimensional heterogeneous panel data is underpowered, especially when cross-sectional dimension exceeds time dimension or signals are sparse.
Approach. PE-PGCT enhances existing panel Granger causality tests with a power-enhancement component that maintains the null distribution while boosting power under alternatives.
Contribution. Theoretically guarantees asymptotic behavior with negligible size distortion under null and significant power gains under alternatives on high-dimensional and sparse panel data.

ICML 2026

Optimal Top-$k$ Identification from Pairwise Comparisons

Problem. Fixed-confidence top-k identification from noisy pairwise comparisons requires an algorithm that minimizes sample complexity while being asymptotically optimal.
Approach. A primal-dual procedure learns the asymptotically optimal comparison allocation online and constructs an adaptive comparison-allocation algorithm that tracks this allocation.
Contribution. First asymptotically optimal algorithm for top-k identification from pairwise comparisons under latent utility models.

On the Sharp Input-Output Analysis of Nonlinear Systems under Adversarial Attacks

Problem. Learning input-output mappings of nonlinear systems is challenging under adversarial attacks with correlated, nonzero-mean disturbances.
Approach. Reformulate the system as a linear combination of basis functions and prove the ℓ2-norm estimator overcomes adversarial attacks when attack times are sparse.
Contribution. Provides sharp input-output analysis and proves optimality by constructing matching lower bounds for nonlinear systems under probabilistic adversarial attacks.

Problem. Weak-to-strong generalization scaling laws are unclear when strong students are trained on imperfect labels from weak teachers.
Approach. Derive deterministic equivalent for excess test error of students trained on teacher labels in random features ridge regression, identifying improvement regimes.
Contribution. Shows scaling law improvement is substantial and students may achieve minimax optimal rate regardless of teacher's scaling law.

Multi-View Causal Discovery without Non-Gaussianity: Identifiability and Algorithms

Problem. Causal discovery typically relies on strong assumptions like non-Gaussianity; modern applications with multiple related views of the same system have rarely been leveraged for causal discovery.
Approach. Propose multi-view linear Structural Equation Model extending non-Gaussian disturbances framework by leveraging correlation across views; prove identifiability for acyclic SEMs.
Contribution. Multi-view methods inspired by DirectLiNGAM, PairwiseLiNGAM, and ICA-LiNGAM enable causal graph estimation from neuroimaging data.

Low-dimensional topology of deep neural networks

Problem. Understanding how neural networks change topological invariants through layers and comparing expressivity of different architectures requires controlled low-dimensional analysis.
Approach. Study layered models by limiting each layer width to d=3 (ℝ³ representation space) enabling visualization and tracking topological invariants including linking numbers through layers.
Contribution. Prove ResNet layer-skipping and transformer attention match in power for affecting linking numbers; both strictly more powerful than feedforward networks but require nonmonotonic activations.

Problem. Training Kolmogorov-Arnold Networks lacks principled theory for training dynamics, generalization, and privacy properties compared to standard MLPs.
Approach. Analyze gradient descent for two-layer KANs with specialization to logistic loss under NTK-separable assumption; derive bounds characterizing optimization, generalization, and differential privacy.
Contribution. Show polylogarithmic width suffices for 1/T optimization and 1/n generalization; identify qualitative gap between non-private and private training regimes revealing polylogarithmic width necessity.

Problem. Knowledge transfer via distillation and weak-to-strong generalization appear disparate; a unified theory explaining when and why transfer works across regimes is missing.
Approach. Establishes unified spectral analysis of SGD dynamics in high-dimensional linear regression, characterizing KT via spectral horizon expansion in KD and spectral denoising in W2S.
Contribution. Reveals efficacy is governed by interplay between implicit regularization and heterogeneous spectral learning speeds, unifying seemingly different transfer phenomena.

Turning Back Without Forgetting: Selective Backward Refinement for Parameter-Efficient Continual Learning

Problem. Prompt-based parameter-efficient continual learning mitigates catastrophic forgetting by isolating task-specific prompts, but limits backward knowledge transfer from later to earlier tasks.
Approach. SABER determines when backward refinement is beneficial using prompt-gradient geometry and loss-distribution similarity, then restricts updates to non-interfering prompt parameter directions.
Contribution. SABER consistently achieves positive backward transfer while maintaining strong overall average performance across continual learning benchmarks on T5-Large, LLaMA, and Qwen.

From Generalist to Specialist Representation

Problem. Learning task-relevant specialist representations from generalist models has unknown fundamental limits on identifiability.
Approach. Completely nonparametric identifiability analysis proves task structure is identifiable across time steps without temporal dependence assumptions, and task-relevant latent representations are identifiable within each step under simple sparsity regularization.
Contribution. Establishes the first general nonparametric identifiability guarantees for moving from generalist to specialist representations across time and task dimensions.

ICML 2026

No Free Lunch: Non-Asymptotic Analysis of Prediction-Powered Inference

Problem. Prediction-Powered Inference shows asymptotic free lunch but finite-sample behavior remains unclear, leaving practitioners uncertain about when to deploy PPI.
Approach. Exact finite-sample analysis of PPI++ on mean estimation characterizes the correlation threshold for pseudo-labels that determines improvement over gold-standard labels alone.
Contribution. For Gaussian data, correlation must exceed 1/sqrt(n-2) for improvement; non-asymptotic variance expressions enable practitioners to reason transparently about PPI++ benefits in specific applications.

Estimating Continuous Treatment Effects with Two-Stage Kernel Ridge Regression

Problem. Estimating continuous treatment effects is complicated by confounding where treatment assignment depends on covariates, creating selection bias that makes direct regression unreliable.
Approach. A two-stage kernel ridge regression method learns a model for response as a function of treatment and covariates, then constructs pseudo-outcomes correcting for distribution shift and fits a second model for treatment effect.
Contribution. Achieves optimal learning bounds without estimating conditional treatment density and includes a fully data-driven model selection procedure with provable adaptivity.

Problem. Diffusion Flow Matching has emerged as a versatile generative framework but its theoretical convergence properties remain only partially understood, particularly regarding discretization error.
Approach. Refined convergence guarantees for Brownian motion based DFMs are provided under Kullback-Leibler divergence and 2-Wasserstein distance with improved dimensional dependence under minimal conditions.
Contribution. Derives state-of-the-art scaling in KL convergence bounds and extends analysis to 2-Wasserstein distance with consistent dimensional dependence.

ICML 2026

Attention's forward pass and Frank-Wolfe

Problem. Self-attention dynamics in the zero-temperature regime (hardmax limit) remain theoretically underexplored, particularly regarding convergence and the relationship to finite-temperature behavior.
Approach. Analysis of hardmax self-attention relates it to Frank-Wolfe optimization over convex hull of tokens, establishing convergence rates and dynamic metastability for finite-beta case via Markov chain modeling.
Contribution. Proves sublinear convergence under negative semidefinite key-query matrices and exponential convergence with Voronoi structure under positive semidefinite case; establishes hardmax accuracy over exponentially long horizons.

ICML 2026

Reliable Confidence Alignment for Generalized Category Discovery

Problem. Generalized Category Discovery requires discovering novel classes from unlabeled data while minimizing performance decay on known classes, yet parametric methods suffer from confidence bias and distributional wedges.
Approach. Reliable Confidence Alignment (RCA) grounds high certainty anchors on labeled data via Reliable Anchor for Certainty (RAC) and propagates reliability to unlabeled discovery set through Cross-view Confidence Alignment (CCA).
Contribution. Effectively rectifies confidence landscape and significantly mitigates performance decay on known classes without compromising novel-class discovery.

ICML 2026

Addressing Instrument-Outcome Confounding in Mendelian Randomization through Representation Learning

Problem. Mendelian Randomization relies on untestable instrumental variable assumptions that are often violated in practice when genetic variants correlate with environmental factors, leading to confounding between instruments and outcomes.
Approach. A representation learning framework exploits multi-environment genetic data to recover latent exogenous components of genetic instruments suitable for causal inference.
Contribution. The approach provides theoretical insights into when learned components act as valid instruments and demonstrates effectiveness through simulations and semi-synthetic experiments using the All of Us Biobank.
